## The Svapnohana's spellings on the prints at full size (q834, q864, q870, q873, q874, q889, q890, q813, q814, q825): the fourth-watch line «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་བར་དུ/པར་བྱ», «བརྒྱད་པ་ལ/བརྒྱད་བར་ལ», «བགྲོད་མིན/བཟློག་མིན», «འགྲོན་བུ», the Gāyatrī's «ཏྲི/ཏྲེ», «བླ་མོ/ལ་མོ», the Sanskrit title's stacks on the Peking (img. 315 l. 1) and the Co ne prints (RD353)

*Read: scan for the Peking and Narthang places and the Co ne tries, search snippets for the etexts, 2026-11-10 (RD353); passage svo:p271a above.*

**The row's Peking image group is the wrong volume.** I1KG13215 is the Peking vol. 46 (the Svapnekṣā, RD327); its img. 315 is a page of mantras, not the Svapnohana. The Svapnohana on the Peking is I1KG13197 (vol. 28) img. 315–318, and the title stands in img. 315 line 1.

*The Co ne (q864, q870, q873; I1GS66059 img. 270, 272, 273).* The BDRC frame was fetched again: the image service answers `--width 4000` with empty files, and the plain fetch is byte for byte the scan already in the cache (2055 × 486 px, bitonal), so there is no sharper Co ne scan. Line 3–4 of img. 270 (the three watch lines) and line 6 end («བགྲོད་/བཟློག་མིན»), line 7 («འགྲོན་བུ/དུ») were cut at 3–7×, deskewed by −0.75° and blurred 1.2–2 px with a contrast stretch: the strokes are broken into dots, the stretch of the fourth-watch line shows its bar-separated segments and the stack «ཐུན» and nothing else, and the end of line 6 and line 7 show rules and specks without letters. No letter of the five doubtful words was told apart; RD276's votes stand unchanged. Nothing was added by Yigdzin-1 beyond RD276 and RD311's sixteen runs.

*The fourth watch's ending (q870, q873).* No witness anywhere on BDRC reads «པར་དུ» or «པར་བྱ»: the exact phrases «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་པར་དུ», «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་ཕྱེད་པར་དུ» and «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་པར་བྱ» give no hit in any etext, while «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་བར་དུ» gives eight: the Derge etext (twice, MW23703_1748 and 1749, the verse stands under both works), four Narthang etext pages (MW2KG5015_1400–1402 and 1416, the same page of the manuscript's etext filed under neighbouring works), Bya mdo Klu byams rgyal and Padma rdo rje. «པར་བྱ» is thus the machine's reading of the Co ne (Yigdzin-1 1.5×) and no printed or etext witness has it; the Co ne's own ending stays unseen.

*The second watch's first segment (q874).* «ཟླ་བ་བརྒྱད་བར་ལ» stands in four texts: the Derge etext (twice), Bya mdo and Padma rdo rje; the Narthang etext (machine text, poor) reads «brgyad pal», that is «བརྒྱད་པ་ལ», as the Narthang manuscript does on the scan (RD280) and the Peking (RD327); the Dpe bsdur ma's notes record Peking and Narthang «པ» against the base (RD323). So «པ་ལ» is the two Narthang witnesses' and the Peking's, «བར་ལ» the Derge etext's and the modern books' (which quote the Derge); which is older is not told by any witness, and the Co ne's is not seen. The Derge scan's 8 px letters were not cut again.

*The Peking title (q834, q890).* After «རྒྱ་གར་སྐད་དུ།» five stacks, read at 4–7× on I1KG13197 img. 315 line 1: the first «སྭ» (ས over the hooked ཝ), the second a head like a box with horizontal strokes over a subjoined ན-like foot (the same box as the པ of «བརྟག་པ» and the བ before it on the line, so པ and བ are not told apart), the third «ཨུ» (clear), then two more stacks. Yigdzin-1 on the title line gives «སྭབྣ་ཨུ་ཧཱ་ནཱ», the same as its reading of the Narthang strip (RD289): so the two prints agree on the machine's text, the second stack's first letter (པ or བ) is not settled by the eye, and no other letter than «བྣ/པྣ» fits the box. The word is then «sva-pna/bna u-hā-nā» with the vowel signs as the machine reads them; that the first two stacks and «ཨུ་ཧ» spell svapna and uha («svapnoha»-like) is a reading of the letters, not of a source. Whether the Sanskrit is svapnoha or svapnoddhāra stays open; no «ད» or «ཌ» stack of «uddhāra» was seen.

*The Gāyatrī (q889).* On the Peking (I1KG13197 img. 318 line 4) the word is three syllables ག, ཡ and a stack «ཏྲ» with a vowel sign above it that is a curved hook, the same form as the ི of «ནི» after it; on the Narthang manuscript (img. 287 line 2) the sign above «ཏྲ» is a curved hook with a loop, and the ེ of «དེ» in the same line is a long straight stroke leaning from the upper left, not this shape. So «ཏྲི» on both, moderately; Yigdzin-1's «ཏྲེ» on the Narthang strip is not supported. The word is three syllables «ག་ཡ་ཏྲི» also in the Derge etext, the Dpe bsdur ma etext (search: «ག་ཡ་ཏྲི་ནི་སྟོང་ཕྲག་བཟླས», three hits) and, by RD276, not in the Co ne's two-syllable «གས་ཏྲི». The three syllables ga ya tri carry the consonants of gāyatrī; the long ā of the Sanskrit is not marked in the spelling.

*The other places (q813, q814, q825).* No new letter: the Peking, Derge, Narthang manuscript and, in the etexts, the Narthang etext, the Dpe bsdur ma etext and Padma rdo rje read «བགྲོད་མིན་ལ་ཞུགས» (search, 8 hits; «བཟློག་མིན་ལ་ཞུགས» no hit), «འགྲོན་བུ་གླང་དང་བ» (the Derge etext, Padma rdo rje; no «འགྲོན་དུ་…» was searched for the Co ne's sake, since no etext holds it) and «རྟ་བླ་མོ་དང་ལྟོ་འཕྱེ» (the Derge and Dpe bsdur ma etexts and Padma rdo rje; «རྟ་ལ་མོ་དང་ལྟོ་འཕྱེ» no hit anywhere, so the plain-ལ spelling is known only from the Peking and Narthang scans read by eye). Padma rdo rje's book, which quotes the Derge, reads «bgrod min» and «rta bla mo» too. The sense of «བགྲོད་མིན» (q813) and the meaning of «བཅུ་ཡི་བར» (q814) are not touched by any of these; «རྟ་བླ་མོ» as the centipede (q825) stands on RD271's note and on no new reading.

## The Sanskrit side of the dream texts (q866, q868, q869, q871): the Brahmavaivarta's dream chapter and its date against the Svapnohana's translation of about 1200, the puṇyakāṇḍa of the Kāṇva branch, Bṛhaspati's Svapnādhyāya (Penn Ms. Coll. 390 item 1871; Negelein's edition) on the three fortnights and the month of the fourth watch (RD357)

*Read: scan, 2026-11-10 (RD357), on page images: Penn Ms. Coll. 390 Items 1871, 2730, 687 and 673 on Penn's own IIIF images (Colenda, 2670 px, which answer at once where the OPenn server of RD277 gave 1 kB/s), the Jivananda print of the Brahmavaivarta (leaves n667–n668), the 1935 Gorkha Pustakalaya print with a Nepali commentary (leaves n6–n8), Bolling and von Negelein's Atharva-pariśiṣṭa 68 (leaves n223, n233, n234) and Negelein 1912, Introduction p. XV (leaf n20). The passages are written into the corpus file of their own source; this section is the reading's account and its answers.*

The passages: [pn1871:i1](../pn1871/pn1871.md#pn1871-i1), [pn1871:i2b](../pn1871/pn1871.md#pn1871-i2b), [pn1871:i4](../pn1871/pn1871.md#pn1871-i4) (the Penn Item 1871: the table of gifts on f. 1r, the first text leaf, the end and the colophon), [pn2730:i0](../pn2730/pn2730.md#pn2730-i0), [pn687:i1](../pn687/pn687.md#pn687-i1), [pn673:i6](../pn673/pn673.md#pn673-i6), [bvp:p660](../bvp/bvp.md#bvp-p660), [bvg:p1-2](../bvg/bvg.md#bvg-p1-2), [bvg:p3](../bvg/bvg.md#bvg-p3), [apv:p445](../apv/apv.md#apv-p445) (re-read on the scan), [apv:p456](../apv/apv.md#apv-p456), [neg:i20](../neg/neg.md#neg-i20). The claims: penn-svapnadhyaya-four-copies-month-for-fourth-watch, penn-svapnadhyaya-opens-with-apv-68-2-58, brahmavaivarta-kanva-tathaiva-tatraiva, sources-own-dates-dream-texts.

### q868 and q871: the Penn copies of the Bṛhaspati Svapnādhyāya

- **Item 1871** (f. 1v, the full first text leaf at 2×): «द्वितीये चाष्टभिर्मासैस्त्रिभिर्मासैस्त्रियामके । चतुर्थे प्रहरे स्वप्नो मासैकेन विपच्यते । अरुणोदयवेलायां दशाहेन फलं भवेत् । गोविसर्जनवेलायां सद्यः स्वप्नो विधीयते ॥»: the second watch eight months, the third three months, the fourth **a month**, dawn ten days, the cows' release at once. The colophon on f. 4r, on the image: «इति बृहस्पत्युक्तस्वप्नाध्यायः समाप्तः», then the Navagrahastotra. The table on f. 1r is a table of gifts by lunar mansion, with no dream in it.
- **Item 2730**, **Item 687**, **Item 673** (colophon, saṃvat 1881 = 1824, read on the image): a month in every copy; Item 687 has **six** months for the second watch and had left out the fourth, written back in the margin as «यामे चतुर्थे मासैकं …».
- **q868** is answered: the Penn Item 1871 does not read Negelein's i («tripakṣake») nor «half a month»; it reads eight months, three months (triyāmake) and a month, the series of Negelein's a and f. **q871** is answered for the Penn copies (a month in all four) and not decided as to which reading is older: the Brahmavaivarta has the half-month in both its prints, the Svapnādhyāya copies a month, and no manuscript of either is older than the 18th century; the one scribal event seen, the marginal repair of Item 687, restores a month.
- The opening line of the Penn copies is the Atharva-pariśiṣṭa's: «svapnas tu prathame yāme saṃvatsara-…» (all the manuscripts of Bolling and von Negelein read *svapnas*, apv:p456; Item 687 down to «saṃvatsara-vipākinaḥ»), so the family's start is older than the Penn copies' century by the Parisiṣṭa; its second span is eight months in every manuscript of the editors.

### q866: the Kāṇva branch's «puṇyakāṇḍa»

The Jivananda print reads «**तथैव** काण्वशाखायां पुण्यकाण्डे मनोहरे» ("likewise in the Kāṇva branch", bvp:p660), the Śabdakalpadruma and the 1935 print «**तत्रैव** …» ("in that same one", the Sāmaveda), which puts the Kāṇva branch under the Sāmaveda and is the source of the oddity noted before. The Nepali commentator of 1935 reads «kaṇvaśākhā» as a section (khaṇḍa) of the Sāmaveda and names no book. The question *which book* is not answered: no print or commentary read names it.

### q869: the age of the dream chapter, and Scheuermann

Nothing read dates the chapter alone; Jagaddeva (neg:i20) gives no date; the oldest copies read of the Brahmavaivarta chapter are the prints of 1888 and 1935. Scheuermann's chapter was again refused to the browser (Brill, HTTP 405); the LMU repository record says no full text is held there. The 1935 commentator glosses the half-month as fifteen days (bvg:p3). Open.

### Open (RD357)

- Question: is the Brahmavaivarta's v. 2 «tathaiva» or «tatraiva» in the manuscripts of the Kṛṣṇajanmakhaṇḍa (a manuscript of the chapter, Bengali or Devanagari, decides it)?
- Question: do the Svapnādhyāya copies older than the 18th century, if there are any (Nepal, the Bhandarkar and Poona collections, the Hamburg NGMCP's «Svapnacintāmaṇi» A 433-50 is another text), read a month, half a month or three fortnights?
- Question: what is Item 687's marginal line «yāme caturthe māsaikaṃ …» in full (its last words), and is the fourth-watch month a corrector's own wording or a copy of another manuscript's?

## Who wrote the Svapnohana, how its watches are bounded, and where its series of spans comes from (q796, q798, q799, q847, q853): whether «ངག་དབང» at the end of its remedy is Vāgīśvarakīrti and what the Tengyur's colophons and catalogues (Derge, Peking, Narthang, Co ne dkar chag, Bu ston's Chos 'byung, Tāranātha) say of its translator and author, how Padma rdo rje introduces his whole quotation of it (pp. 17–26 of his book) and whether he glosses the watches, what the Tibetan commentaries and medical or divination books say of the four watches' bounds, and whether an older Indian text has the series year, eight months, three months, half a month, ten days (RD359)

*Read: etext, 2026-11-10 (RD359): BDRC etexts of the Derge Tengyur's catalogue volume (vol. 213, VE23703_1531), the Jo nang Tengyur catalogue (VEIE0OPI28138A6F_I1KG9048), Bu ston's Chos 'byung (VE1ALS12060E), the Narthang manuscript's catalogue volume (vol. 225, I2KG212509, OCR too corrupt to read), and BDRC exact-phrase searches (about 40 phrases, Tibetan script); no scan of the catalogues was served (Derge vol. 213: HTTP 500; Jo nang: 401), no model run. Passages: [svo:i736](#svo-i736), [svo:i64](#svo-i64), [svo:i409](#svo-i409) (named by etext page, the catalogues having no printed page in the pool's registry).*

**What was found.**

1. q796. The Derge catalogue (Tshul khrims rin chen's, in the print's vol. 213) lists the Examination of Dreams right after the Deceiving Death, as Vibhūticandra's own translation, with no author; the Deceiving Death it gives to Ngag gi dbang phyug grags pa. The Jo nang Tengyur catalogue (Phyogs las rnam rgyal, 2010), which copies the same entries, writes that author's name «ངག་དབང་གྲགས་པ» (Ngag dbang grags pa). So «ངག་དབང» is an attested short form of Vāgīśvarakīrti's name, and the man whose work stands directly before the Svapnohana in the volume. No catalogue says that the remedy's «ངག་དབང» is he: the identification stays probable, now with an attested form of the name (claims/svo-ngag-dbang-grags-pa-short-name.md). The catalogues do not name an author for the Svapnohana; Bu ston, the Derge and the Jo nang catalogues all give "Vibhūticandra's own translation" (claims/svo-catalogues-derge-jo-nang.md).
2. q847. Padma rdo rje's book closes its quotation with the Tengyur's colophon sentence «རྒྱ་གར་ཤར་ཕྱོགས་ཀྱི་པཎྜི་ཏ་བི་བྷཱུ་ཏི་ཙནྡྲས་རང་འགྱུར་དུ་མཛད་པའོ» (BDRC snippet in EWTS, "rgya gar shar phyogs kyi paN+Di ta bi b+hU ti tsan+d+ras rang 'gyur du mdzad pa'o", etext volume VEIE0OPI409E1122_I1KG10372, the chunk at char. 25516 that follows «rmi lam brtag pa rdzogs so»): he copies the colophon, so his quotation names Vibhūticandra and no other. The sentence that introduces the quotation was still not reached (below).
3. q798. No catalogue, colophon or phrase search gives a gloss of the four watches' bounds: «ཐུན་བཞི་ཟླ་བ་ཕྱེད་བར་དུ» and the other lines of the verse are found only in the Derge, Narthang and Dpe bsdur ma volumes of the Svapnohana (which also print it inside the Tārā sādhanas of the Narthang etext's misaligned OCR), Padma rdo rje's book and Bya mdo Klu byams rgyal's (RD262); the searches for the first, second and fourth watch with «རྨི་ལམ» in the Tibetan order gave no hit. Open as before.
4. q799 and q853: nothing new; the lexica of RD322 and the Indian texts of RD312 and RD357 are the readings that bear on them.

### Open (RD359)

- Question: What sentence introduces Padma rdo rje's quotation of the Svapnohana (his pp. 16–17, etext chars about 18600–21345, the paragraphs between the account of the dream's length and the quotation's first line), and does he name the Tengyur, the translator or an author there? (q847 unchanged; the quotation's close names Vibhūticandra by the colophon.)
- Question: Does the Narthang Tengyur catalogue («Bstan pa chos 'gyur ro cog gi dkar chag tshangs pa'i dbyangs», vol. 225 of the Tibet House manuscript, I2KG212509; the etext is unreadable, the entry probably near etext pp. 168 and 200, where «འཆི་བ་བླུ་བ» stands) list the Svapnohana, and with what words for its maker?

### Searched and not found (RD359)

- Derge catalogue vol. 213 (I1531) images 736–737, and the Derge vol. 28 image 268, answered HTTP 500 on 2026-11-10 (the first only after earlier 429s); the catalogue entries are etext only.
- The Jo nang catalogue scans (I1KG9048): 401 (lent only).
- The Peking catalogue and the Co ne catalogue: no etext or scan was located in this session (the Peking vols. 221–224 of BDRC's W1KG13126 were not opened; the Co ne W1GS66030 has no catalogue entry of the work).
- Tāranātha: no search string of the work's title with Vibhūticandra's name found it; his history names translators, not works.
- Phrases «ངག་དབང་གྲགས་པས་མཛད་པའི་རྨི་ལམ», «ངག་གི་དབང་ཕྱུག་གྲགས་པའི་རྨི་ལམ», «རྨི་ལམ་ཐུན་བཞི», «ཐུན་དང་པོར་རྨི་ལམ», «ཞག་བཅུའི་ནང་དུ་འབྲས་བུ་འབྱིན» (only the known books), «བསྟན་འགྱུར་རྨི་ལམ་བརྟག་པ», «རྨི་ལམ་བརྟག་པ་ལས་ཀྱང»: no new hit. «མཚན་ཐུན་བཞི» and «མཚན་ཐུན་དང་པོ» have 21 and 25 hits, none a dream text in the listed ones (the first eight of each not opened one by one).
- The older Indian text with the series: not looked for again; RD312 and RD357 hold it.

## <a id="svo-i736"></a>svo:i736 The Examination of Dreams in the Derge Tengyur catalogue (vol. 213, etext pp. 736 and 765): translator only, set after the Deceiving Death (RD359)

*Read: etext, 2026-11-10.*

> > འཆི་བ་བསླུ་བའི་མན་ངག་ཅེས་བྱ་བ་སློབ་དཔོན་ངག་གི་དབང་ཕྱུག་གྲགས་པས་མཛད་པ། ཤླཽ་ཀ་སུམ་བརྒྱ་དྲུག་ཅུ་པ། རྒྱ་གར་གྱི་མཁན་པོ་དཱི་པཾ་ཀ་ར་ཤྲཱི་ཛྙཱ་ན་དང༌། ལོ་ཙཱ་བ་དགེ་སློང་རིན་ཆེན་བཟང་པོའི་འགྱུར། འདི་ཕྱི་ནང་གི་རྒྱུད་དུ་མ་དང་འབྲེལ་ཡང༌། ཁྱད་པར་མ་རྒྱུད་རྡོ་རྗེ་མཁའ་འགྲོ་དང༌། གདན་བཞི་དང་འབྲེལ་པར་སྣང་བས་འདིར་བཀོད་དོ། །རྨི་ལམ་བརྟག་པ་རྒྱ་གར་ཤར་ཕྱོགས་ཀྱི་པཎྜི་ཏ་བི་བྷཱུ་ཏི་ཙནྡྲའི་རང་འགྱུར། པདྨ་དྲ་བ་འབྱུང་བའི་སྒྲུབ་ཐབས་རྒྱལ་བ་དཔལ་ཡེ་ཤེས་ཀྱིས་མཛད་པ་དང༌། …
> >
> > (the same catalogue, a page later, in the list of Advayavajra's works:) …རང་བཞིན་དུ་གནས་པའི་སྔགས་ཀྱི་སྤྱོད་པ་བཅུ་པ། རྨི་ལམ་བརྟག་པ། སངས་རྒྱས་བྱང་ཆུབ་བསམ་གཏན་གྱི་དུག་སེལ་བར་བྱེད་པའི་མར་མེ་ཞེས་བྱ་བ། …རྣམས་རྒྱ་གར་གྱི་མཁན་པོ་དཔལ་ལྡན་གཉིས་སུ་མེད་པའི་རྡོ་རྗེས་མཛད་པ། རྣལ་འབྱོར་པ་བསོད་སྙོམས་པ་པྲཛྙཱ་ཤྲཱི་ཛྙཱ་ན་ཀཱིརྟིའི་རང་འགྱུར།

Derge Tengyur catalogue (the catalogue volume of the Derge print, vol. 213, BDRC etext VE23703_1531, etext p. 736 and p. 765–766; the author's closing verse names the monk Tshul khrims rin chen who wrote it out at Lhun grub steng). In the section of the mother-tantra works, after the Tārā works: "The Teaching on Deceiving Death ('chi ba bslu ba'i man ngag), made by the teacher Ngag gi dbang phyug grags pa, in three hundred and sixty verses; translated by the Indian abbot Dīpaṃkara-śrījñāna and the monk and lotsāba Rin chen bzang po. Although this is tied to many tantras, outer and inner, it seems tied especially to the mother tantras, the Vajraḍāka and the Four Seats, so it is set down here. The Examination of Dreams (rmi lam brtag pa): the eastern Indian paṇḍita Vibhūticandra's own translation. The Padma-net sādhana made by the Victor Dpal ye shes, and …" (the entry ends there; the next group is the Black Lord's, Mahākāla). A page later, among the works of Advayavajra: "…the Ten-part Mantra Conduct of Natural Abiding, the Examination of Dreams, the Lamp that Removes the Poison of Meditation…, all made by the Indian abbot, the glorious Advayavajra; Prajñāśrījñānakīrti's own translation" (this is Advayavajra's own dream work, the Svapnekṣā).

Which entry gives which fact: the Svapnohana's line names one man, Vibhūticandra, with «རང་འགྱུར» (own translation, rang 'gyur) and no author; it stands directly after the Deceiving Death and before the Padma-net sādhana, the order of the Derge volume itself; the catalogue says of the Deceiving Death alone that it was set in the mother tantras by the cataloguer's own judgement. The remedy's «ངག་དབང» is not mentioned. State: etext (the Derge catalogue's scans were not served, HTTP 500, 2026-11-10); the Tibetan is the etext's, not read on a page image.
